## Document 004: 115年8月海關進出口貿易初步統計 (2026-09-09)

Report source: MOF
Date: 2026-09-09
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

2 
二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

## Document 055: 2026年9月物价数据点评：国际油价延续上行叠加芯片价格上涨，9月国内物价涨幅扩大

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月物价数据点评：国际油价延续上行叠加芯片价格上涨，9月国内物价涨幅扩大.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
国际油价延续上行叠加芯片价格上涨，9 月国内物价涨幅扩大 
——2026 年 9 月物价数据点评 
研究发展部执行总监 冯琳 
事件：根据国家统计局10 月14 日公布的数据，2026 年9 月，CPI 同比上涨0.9%，上月为上涨0.8%，
1-9 月 CPI 累计同比为 0.9%，2025 年 CPI 累计同比为 0.0%；9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%，上月为上涨 3.8%，
1-9 月累计同比上涨2.3%，2025 年累计同比下降2.6%。 
基本观点：9 月中东局势升级，国际油价再度上涨，推动国内汽油价格上调，是当月 CPI 同比涨幅扩
大的主要原因。另外，受全球AI 投资热潮带动芯片价格大幅上冲影响，9 月国内电脑、手机等电子产品价
格继续上涨，对整体物价水平也有推升作用。不过，伴随促消费政策效应有所减弱，9 月汽车、家电等耐
用消费品价格走低，是限制当月 CPI 涨幅的一个主要因素。9 月剔除变动较大的能源和食品价格，更能反
映基本物价水平的核心 CPI 同比为 1.0%，继续处于偏低水平，背后主要是当前消费市场仍然呈现明显的供
强需弱特征。预计10 月CPI 同比涨幅会在1.0%左右，年底前会持续处于温和偏低状态。9 月PPI 环比涨势
加快，同比涨幅明显扩大，主要受国际输入性因素影响。当月国际原油、有色金属价格上行并向国内相关
产业传导，提振国内石油、有色金属产业链价格普遍上涨。同时，基建活动回暖叠加供给端限制带动国内
煤炭、钢铁等基础原材料价格回升，以及AI 投资热潮推动半导体产业链价格上涨等因素，对9 月PPI 涨势
也起到助推作用。预计10 月PPI 同比涨幅会扩大至5.2%左右，上行斜率明显放缓，后期有波动下行趋势。 
具体来看： 
一、9 月中东局势升级，国际油价再度上涨，推动国内汽油价格上调，是当月 CPI 同比涨幅扩大的主
要原因。另外，受全球AI 投资热潮带动芯片价格大幅上冲影响，9 月国内电脑、手机等电子产品价格继续
上涨，对整体物价水平也有推升作用。不过，伴随促消费政策效应有所减弱，9 月汽车、家电等耐用消费
品价格走低，是限制当月 CPI 涨幅的一个主要因素。9 月剔除变动较大的能源和食品价格，更能反映基本
物价水平的核心CPI 同比为1.0%，继续处于偏低水平，背后主要是当前消费市场仍然呈现明显的供强需弱
特征。 
9 月 CPI 环比上行0.2%，符合季节性规律，同比涨幅扩大至0.9%，较上月扩大0.1 个百分点。背后的
主要推动因素是在当月中东冲突升温、国际油价再度上涨背景下，国内汽油价格上涨4.9%，叠加上年同期

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
价格基数下降，9 月汽油价格同比涨幅达到16.6%，比上月扩大7.3 个百分点；仅此一项就影响9 月CPI 同
比上涨 0.47 个百分点，影响幅度比上月扩大0.19 个百分点，是推动当月整体CPI 同比涨幅略有扩大的主
要原因。此外，受全球 AI 投资热潮带动芯片价格上涨影响，9 月国内手机、计算机、平板电脑、数据存储
设备等电子产品价格继续上涨，价格同比涨幅都有不同程度扩大，也对9 月整体 CPI 同比有一定推升作用。 
不过，当前以旧换新政策对汽车、家电等耐用消费品价格支撑作用减弱，9 月这两类补贴类耐用消费
品价格环比下行，叠加上年同期基数走高，价格同比分别下降1.7%和1.8%，降幅较上月扩大0.1 和0.9 个
百分点，是制约当月 CPI 同比涨幅的一个主要原因。其他方面，受美联储加息带动，9 月国际金价大幅下
跌，国内金饰品价格同步走低，价格同比涨幅从上月的33.6%回落到22.5%。 
食品价格方面，受中秋、国庆假期需求增加等影响，9 月猪肉、蔬菜、水果价格环比上行，猪肉价格
同比降幅收窄。不过，上年同期基数偏高影响下，当月蔬菜、水果价格同比降幅都有所扩大。整体上看，
9 月食品价格同比降幅较上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，达到-1.7%。值得注意的是，受供应仍然偏紧影响，9 月
鸡蛋价格环比高位上行，同比涨幅扩大0.7 个百分点至18.5%。 
服务价格方面，9 月暑期结束，旅游出行消费需求放缓，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格都有季
节性下降，但受上年同期基数偏低影响，当月出行服务价格同比上涨 2.1%，涨幅较上月扩大 0.8 个百分点。
不过，由于教育服务价格同比涨幅回落，9 月服务价格同比涨幅与上月持平，为 0.8%。这也是 9 月扣除波
动较大的食品和能源价格、更能反映整体物价水平的核心CPI 同比涨幅为1.0%，与上月持平的主要原因。 
整体上看，中东局势屡现反复背景下，国际油价波动是当前牵动 CPI 走势的一个主要因素。值得注意
的是，全球AI 投资热潮对国内手机、电脑等商品价格的推升作用正在显现，但后期扩散至电子产品之外的
可能性不大。9 月核心 CPI 同比继续处在 1.0%的低位，显示当前物价涨幅偏低，主要受国内消费需求不振、
市场供强需弱拖累。 
往后看，10 月初国际油价处于高位，当月电子产品价格也会延续上行，我们预计10 月 CPI 环比会继
续上行，同比涨幅会升至1.0%左右，但仍将低于今年2.0%左右的控制目标。整体上看，年底前物价水平都
会处在温和偏弱状态。 
二、9 月 PPI 环比涨势加快，同比涨幅明显扩大，主要受国际输入性因素影响。当月国际原油、有色
金属价格上行并向国内相关产业传导，提振国内石油、有色金属产业链价格普遍上涨。同时，基建活动回
暖叠加供给端限制带动国内煤炭、钢铁等基础原材料价格回升，以及 AI 投资热潮推动半导体产业链价格
上涨等因素，对 9 月PPI 涨势也起到助推作用。

### Page 3

东方金诚宏观研究

3 
9 月 PPI 环比上涨1.2%，涨幅较上月大幅加快0.8 个百分点，带动当月同比涨幅从上月的3.8%扩大至
5.0%，创 2022 年下半年以来新高。可以看到，9 月制造业 PMI 中的主要原材料购进价格指数和出厂价格指
数分别达到 60.8%和54.0%，比上月上升4.2 个和3.6 个百分点，已预示当月PPI 涨幅会进一步扩大。9 月
PPI 涨势加速，主要受三个因素推动： 
一是 9 月中东局势升温，国际原油价格高位上行，国内成品油及化工产业链价格普遍出现较大幅度上
涨。其中，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造价格环比分别上涨14.5%和 7.4%，涨幅较上月加快 4.1 和 3.3 个
百分点，化学原料及化学制品制造、化学纤维制造价格环比分别上涨2.4%和2.1%，涨幅较上月加快2.5 和
1.7 个百分点。 
二是受稳增长政策发力、极端天气影响减退等因素影响，9 月基建活动增加，加之供给端限制，当月
国内主导的煤炭、钢铁、水泥等基础原材料价格全面上涨。其中，煤炭开采、黑色金属冶炼及压延加工、
非金属矿物制品行业价格环比分别上涨 9.5%、0.2%、0.3%，涨幅较上月分别加快 6.7、1.1 和 0.5 个百分
点。 
三是全球AI 投资热潮下，9 月芯片等电子元件价格继续较快上行。同时，AI 投资热潮也带动铜等有色
金属需求释放，叠加供给端约束，9 月有色金属价格总体上行，提振国内有色金属矿采选、有色金属冶炼
及压延加工业价格环比分别上涨1.6%、1.5%，涨幅较上月分别加快2.5 和 0.7 个百分点。 
分大类看，9 月生产资料环比上涨 1.4%，涨幅较上月加快 1.0 个百分点。分项中，采掘工业价格上涨
7.7%（前值 2.7%），原材料工业价格上涨2.6%（前值 1.8%），加工工业价格上涨0.2%（前值 0.0%），显
示上游涨价势头明显强于中下游。同比来看，9 月生产资料价格同比涨幅较上月加快 1.5 个百分点至 6.5%，
分项中，采掘工业、原材料工业、加工工业价格分别上涨25.4%、9.4%和3.4%，涨幅较上月分别加快7.6、
2.7 和0.3 个百分点。 
9 月生活资料价格环比上涨 0.1%，涨幅较上月放缓 0.1 个百分点，表现依然偏弱。从分项来看，食品
价格上涨 0.2%（前值 0.0%）；一般日用品价格环比止跌，从上月的下跌0.2%改善至 0.0%；衣着价格环比
延续 0.0%；耐用消费品价格环比涨幅回落至 0.2%（前值 0.6%），带动当月生活资料价格环比涨幅总体放
缓。值得一提的是，近期耐用消费品价格上涨，可能受上游能源以及有色金属、半导体等原材料涨价带来
的成本压力驱动，这在近期手机等电子产品涨价趋势中得到体现，但汽车价格下跌对耐用消费品价格造成
一定拖累。同比来看，9 月生活资料价格同比下跌 0.1%，跌幅较上月收窄 0.4 个百分点。分项中，食品、
一般日用品价格同比跌幅收窄，衣着价格跌幅与上月持平，仅耐用消费品价格同比上涨，且涨幅在低基数

### Page 4

东方金诚宏观研究

4 
和环比上涨动能共同推动下加快至1.6%（前值1.2%）。 
往后看，考虑到接下来宏观政策将向稳增长方向发力，其中投资增速有望加快，这将对上游原材料价
格有一定拉动作用，而芯片等电子元件价格还会继续上涨。不过，接下来原油价格上行势头有望减弱。综
合以上，我们预计10 月PPI 同比涨幅将在5.2%左右，上行斜率明显放缓，后期有波动下行趋势。

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,459. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

M
Update
King Slide Works Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
Preliminary August Profit and
September Monthly Sales (-31%
MoM/+196% YoY)
Sentiment to stay soft in near term but 2027 outlook is
promising.
King Slide announced its preliminary profit for August, per TWSE regulatory
requirements:
• Revenue was NT$6,466mn (+1% MoM/+344% YoY), 33% of our estimate for
3Q26 of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and 39% of consensus' NT
$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).

• Pretax income was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY; pretax margin at 74.6%), 29%
of our 3Q26 pretax income estimate of NT$16,377mn (margin at 83.7%) and
34% of consensus' NT$14,094mn (margin at 84.1%).
• Net income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY; EPS NT$40.07; NPM 59.1%), 30%
of our 3Q26 net income estimate of NT$12,938mn (EPS NT$135.76; NPM
66.2%) and 34% consensus' NT$11,118mn (EPS NT$114.38; NPM 66.4%).
September monthly sales
• September sales of NT$4,464mn (-31% MoM/+196% YoY), driving 3Q26

revenue to NT$17,338mn (+60% QoQ/+297% YoY), were short of our
estimate of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and but ahead of the
consensus forecast of NT$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).
Our view:
• We think the sequential decline in September  revenue was driven by slower
momentum for some projects, which entered the later part of their product
cycles. While sentiment should stay soft in the near term, we think the
outlook into 2027 is still promising as customers start new product cycles.

• We maintain our view that King Slide's competitiveness in the comprehensive
patent portfolio and strong execution are entrenched and accentuated amid
fast generation migration in AI applications.
• Stay OW with PT of NT$15,000 (30x 2027e P/E).

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Slide Works Co. Ltd. (2059.TW)
Base case, 30x 2027e P/E. We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it better captures King Slide's earnings growth potential. We believe our target mul-
tiple is justified by an operating profit CAGR of 77% seen for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4
vs. the average of 1.2 over 2010-24, when the stock was trading at 17-18x P/E with an operating

profit CAGR of 14%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected AI capex
n Stronger server demand
n Faster market share gains
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Weaker server demand
n Sluggish kitchen furniture demand
n Competition leading to slower share gains.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3

### Page 1

尽管美国 9 ⽉ PPI 环⽐涨幅创四年新⾼、零售销售超预期，本应⽀撑美元⾛强，但 AI 相关资
产遭抛售引发避险情绪，资⾦涌⼊美债致收益率下⾏，反⽽拖累美元承压，周四美元下跌
0.3% 。分析⼈⼠指出，美元与 AI 资本⽀出深度绑定，⼀旦市场担忧 AI 投资触顶，美元将⾸
当其冲。
摘要：
美元周四⼤幅下挫，抹去此前受超预期通胀数据提振的涨幅，⼈⼯智能相关⻛险引发的避险情
绪成为主导⼒量。
周四彭博美元即期指数下跌 0.3% ，创 9 ⽉ 3 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，美元对⼏乎所有主要货币均告
⾛低，其中对瑞郎跌幅约达 1% 。
这⼀⾛势令市场感到困惑，同⽇公布的美国⽣产者价格指数和零售销售数据均⾼于预期，本应
⽀撑美元⾛强，但 AI 板块的⻛险情绪最终主导了市场⽅向。
下⼀个关键信号将来⾃美联储主席沃什。他将于周四纽约时间晚间，在曼⾕举⾏的国际货币基
⾦组织 - 世界银⾏年会上发表讲话。由于美联储将于周六进⼊ 10 ⽉议息会议前的静默期，此次讲
话将是沃什在会议前最后⼀次公开表态机会，市场对其措辞的解读将直接影响利率预期⾛向。

AI ⻛险触发避险买盘，债券收益率⾛低拖累美元
美元此次下跌的导⽕索源于 AI 相关资产的抛售情绪。
Monex 外汇交易员 Andrew Hazlett 表⽰：
美元与 AI 投资逻辑之间的内在关联，使得这⼀板块的动荡对美元的冲击更为直接。
美国银⾏策略师 Alex Cohen 指出， " 市场⽬前处于⼀种颇为奇特的状态 " 。AI 忧虑驱使投资者涌⼊
避险资产，但美元与 AI 资本⽀出的紧密绑定，意味着该板块⼀旦出现问题，美元本⾝也难逃拖
随着 AI 债务遭到抛售，资⾦流向美国国债等避险资产，推低收益率，进⽽对美元形成压制。

累。
Spectra Markets 总裁 Brent Donnelly 在研报中表态更为直⽩：
通胀与零售数据⾛强，美元涨势昙花⼀现
基本⾯数据本应为美元提供强劲⽀撑。
据华尔街⻅闻，美国劳⼯统计局周四公布数据显⽰， 9 ⽉美国⽣产者价格指数环⽐上涨 1.3% ，创
逾四年来最⼤涨幅。零售销售数据亦超出市场预期，受汽油消费提振。
此前⼀天，华尔街⻅闻提及，好于预期的消费者通胀数据已令市场重新燃起对美联储加息的预
期，周三美元⼀度短暂攀升⾄ 2025 年 5 ⽉以来⾼位。
然⽽，这⼀涨势在 AI ⻛险情绪的冲击下迅速⽡解，显⽰出当前市场交易逻辑的复杂性，宏观数
据的指引效⼒正受到主题性⻛险情绪的⼲扰。
市场⽬光随即转向沃什的讲话。在 9 ⽉加息前，沃什曾向市场传递鹰派信号，促使交易员及时调
整加息预期。

尽管沃什曾表⽰不会以⾃⾝的近期货币政策展望来引导市场⾛向，但在通胀数据接连超预期的
背景下，其此番讲话势必受到更⾼强度的审视。
如果市场开始定价 AI 资本开⽀触顶的可能性，美元就是卖出对象。整个美国经济不过是⼀条腿撑
起的独脚凳，⽽这条腿就是 AI 交易。

## Document 156: AI泡沫担忧升温，台积电送来“定心丸”：未来三年资本开支将远超千亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫担忧升温，台积电送来“定心丸”：未来三年资本开支将远超千亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

台积电管理层确认 2026 年资本⽀出 600 ⾄ 640 亿美元，并明确 2026 ⾄ 2028 年累计⽀出将⼤
幅超越过去三年约 1010 亿美元总量。摩根⼤通认为这为上游半导体设备商提供了跨财年的
⻓期订单保障。其中业务⾼度依赖台积电等代⼯客⼾的设备商，将最直接地享受这⼀波资
本开⽀红利。
摘要：
台积电公布强劲三季度业绩，并释放出远超市场预期的未来投资信号。
台积电于 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇公布三季度财报，单季营收达 466 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 16% ，较彭博市场⼀致预
期⾼出约 2% 。公司四季度营收指引中值为 533 亿美元，超出⼀致预期约 6% ，全年增速指引达
44% 。
更受市场关注的是，管理层确认 2026 年资本⽀出维持 600 亿⾄ 640 亿美元区间不变，并进⼀步明
确表⽰ 2026 ⾄ 2028 年三年合计资本⽀出将⼤幅超越 2023 ⾄ 2025 年约 1010 亿美元的历史总量。
追⻛交易台消息， 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇，摩根⼤通 Mio Shikanai 团队发表报告，认为公司管理层不仅维持

了近期投资指引，更将资本⽀出的确定性延伸⾄ 2028 年乃⾄ 2030 年前后，为半导体设备商提供
了超越单⼀财年的⻓期订单能⻅度。
摩根⼤通指出，在晶圆代⼯客⼾营收占⽐较⾼的设备商将从中受益最为直接。在设备需求旺
盛、供应偏紧的背景下，台积电提前与供应商共享投资计划的做法，有助于缓解短期供应链压
⼒，同时也进⼀步巩固了头部设备商的战略地位。
扩产路线清晰，建⼚计划覆盖中国台湾及多个海外市场
台积电管理层披露了详细的产能扩张时间表。在中国台湾，公司计划建设 13 座先进芯⽚及先进
封装晶圆⼚；在海外，则规划新增五⾄六座晶圆⼚。
按制程节点来看，A14 技术预计 2027 年进⼊⻛险试产， 2028 年实现量产。 N2 制程⽅⾯，公司计
划于 2027 年将产能翻倍。 N3 制程的扩产⼒度超出此前预期。
台南原有新⼚计划于 2027 年上半年投产，但因客⼾需求强劲，管理层决定额外新建⼀座台南⼯
⼚，预计 2028 年上半年开始量产。
海外⽅⾯，美国亚利桑那州第⼆座晶圆⼚建设已完⼯，⽬前正处于设备安装阶段，管理层计划

于 2027 年三季度开始量产，进度略超原定计划。
⽇本熊本第⼆座晶圆⼚（ JASM ）的 N3 制程量产则预计于 2028 年启动。公司整体亦持续推进由
N5 向 N3 的全⾯切换。
资本⽀出超越历史区间， 2030 年前后或再度跃升
尽管 2026 年资本开⽀区间维持 600 亿⾄ 640 亿美元不变，管理层就中⻓期⽀出⾛势释放了更为积
极的信号。
台积电明确表⽰， 2026 ⾄ 2028 年累计资本开⽀将⼤幅⾼于过去三年约 1010 亿美元的总量，显⽰
出更为陡峭的投资加速曲线。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

管理层还前瞻性地提及，公司计划于 2030 年前后开始引⼊⾼数值孔径极紫外（ High-NA EUV ）
光刻设备，届时资本⽀出⽔平可能显著⾼于当前。
⽬前设备、材料及劳动⼒成本均呈快速上涨态势，但管理层表⽰，随着投资稳步推进，营收增
速应能持续超越资本⽀出增速。

## Document 215: Daiwa_20261007_Acter 5536

Source PDF: all report pdf/Daiwa_20261007_Acter 5536.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

7 October 2026

Acter

Initiation: acting on the demand for cleanrooms

➢ Fast-growing order backlog likely to be realised over 2026-28E 
➢ Initiating with a Buy (1) rating and a 12-month TP of TWD1,415

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides 
integrated cleanroom 
solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms 
Acter's order backlog has increased from TWD55bn in 1Q26 to TWD60bn in 2Q26 and 
TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth 
drivers. Acter serves as a contractor for both Taiwanese and overseas OSAT providers, 
including TWD21.7bn contracts YTD, awarded by a major OSAT client vs. TWD16.6bn 
contract obtained from the same client in 2025. In cloud infrastructure, Acter maintains 
qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

The supply of cleanroom 
services is limited due to 
high entry barriers in the 
segment

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

Acter: past-3-year-forward PER (average: 12.0x)    Acter: past-5-year-forward PER (average: 9.5x)

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates  Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call: 
weaker-than-expected 
expansion in 
semiconductor industry, 
project delays or 
cancellations, and 
higher-than-expected 
raw-material price hikes

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Appendix 
Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.

Comprehensive 
cleanroom EPCM 
provider in Taiwan; 
dominates both high-
tech market and 
consumer industry

### Page 20

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acter: global footprint

Source: Company, Daiwa

In 2Q26, Acter classified revenue as: cleanroom electromechanical (63%), water, gas and 
chemical delivery systems (28%), consumer industry electromechanical (6%), biomedical 
(2%) and high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (1%). In 2025, the product mix 
was: cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas and chemical delivery systems 
(20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and high-tech 
equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

Acter serves a diversified client base divided into high-tech and consumer divisions. The 
high-tech division focuses on advanced sectors including semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, AI 
data centres (for CSPs), and electronics assembly. The consumer division extends into 
healthcare, premium commercial properties, logistics facilities, and green engineering. This 
multi-industry strategy allows the company to capitalise on tech sector upgrades while 
maintaining resilient operations across technology (especially semiconductor) cycles.

Acter adopts a management strategy involving local hiring in overseas markets, prioritising 
collaboration with local supply chains and compliance with local regulations to reduce 
cross-border deployment costs and thus build strong local teams. Acter has more than 
2,600 employees worldwide (average age: 42 years; average length of service: 7 years). 
With more than 45 years of experience in EPCM, the company plays a reliable role for both 
high-tech and consumer industry.

technological innovation. As a green engineering provider, the company develops energy-
saving construction methods, digital twins, ice-storage energy conservation, wastewater 
recycling, and AI smart facility management systems to help clients achieve carbon 
neutrality while expanding capacity. Backed by solid corporate governance, Acter

Glossary of terms 
EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management): Cleanroom 
construction services that cover the whole process including project design, purchasing, 
construction and management.

BIM (building information modelling): A digital modelling system of a building and its key 
information used in cleanroom construction.

C&S (civil & structural): Site infrastructure and the design and construction of load-
bearing elements.

MEP (mechanical, electrical, and plumbing): Building essential systems for environment 
control, power, lighting, water, and drainage.

Acter maintains a structured governance framework that supports disciplined management. The

Capital management 1
Acter manages capital in a stable and shareholder-friendly manner. Over the past several years,
Acter has typically maintained a dividend payout ratio of 65-80%. It also maintains a strong
financing network across multiple banks, supporting flexibility for project execution and
expansion while preserving financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Acter’s business is supported by a transparent network of suppliers, project partners, and
financial counterparties, including companies with equity ties and reciprocal business
relationships that are common in its operating ecosystem. These relationships are managed
through formal internal procedures for fund lending, endorsements and guarantees, and asset
acquisition and disposal, all of which are approved through shareholder mechanisms and
company rules. Such a structure helps keep inter-company and supply-chain transactions
organised and traceable, reducing the risk of unclear dealings.
E Product quality &safety 1
Acter executes its projects through a complete, disciplined process covering bidding
assessment, project planning, design, procurement, construction management, verification and
testing, completion and acceptance, closing review, and warranty service. Its cleanroom and
electromechanical engineering business is built around maintaining temperature, humidity, and
dust-free environments that support high-precision manufacturing. The company strengthens
quality control through BIM, Revit, simulation software, ISO drawing output tools, and automated
modeling, which improve design accuracy, construction efficiency, and project consistency.
E Ecological impacts 1
Acter has built a concrete sustainability programme with measurable environmental outcomes.
The company completed Scope 1, 2, and 3 greenhouse gas emissions calculation, obtained
third-party verification under ISO 14064-1:2018, and publicly disclosed energy consumption and
emissions data. It also implemented a strong waste reduction programme, achieving a 95%
wastepaper recycling rate at headquarters in 2025 and 100% paper recycling at the 19F and 20F
floors after establishing paper segregation and reuse procedures. The company also promoted
sustainability measures through emissions inventory management, environmental disclosures,
and resource-efficiency practices. These actions show active environmental management and a
clear commitment to lowering operational impact.

## Document 249: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.
